 Dancefloors & You & You (5:31)
 Review: Earlier this year, Berlin scene stalwart Johannes Albert - a producer known for blending nostalgic house, deep house, acid house and nu-disco sounds - launched the Golden Seconds imprint as an outlet for his more disco-focused cuts. He begins strongly with A-side 'Covered Call', a slowly morphing, loop-fired chunk of disco house that sits somewhere between classic French Touch and German don Sound Stream. On the flip, 'Real Dynamics' is a more loose-limbed but no less loop-fired chunk of housed-up , acid-flecked disco gold based around samples from a killer disco-funk workout, while 'Dancefloors & You & You' is a deeper amd more low-slung chunk of warming disco-house pleasure. … Read more

 That Heat (4:53)
 Review: Detroit's Dez Andres has many skills in his arsenal - he's a deep digger who plays wonderfully eclectic live stream sets from home, he is a hip-hop producer with a lovely lived-in sound, and he also crafts hooky deep house. His next trick is this sumptuous EP on Ben Hixon's superb Dolfin, and it fits in perfectly with the label's dusty, heads-down MO. 'Get Down' is a low-riding and bumping house cut with a nagging vocal hook, 'Hey' has signature Dez slouch to the drums and 'McArthur' brings a funky bassline and brighter synth stabs. 'Hands' is loopy and uptempo and full of squelchy synth magic while 'That Heat' has a more live and instrumental energy laced into MPC-style beats. … Read more

 Pattern 01
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1183281-01-02-01.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1183281-01-02-01.mp3] 
 Pattern 02
 Review: As you'd expect from an 'edits' release featuring sneaky tech-house remixes, there's a fair amount of mystery surrounding the first missive from the previously unheard Axis Groove. So, what's on offer? Well, A-side 'Pattern 01' takes familiar elements from an Essex-rooted 1980s dark synth-pop outfit and turns it into a chunky, propulsive and rolling slab of tech-tinged house excellent. In contrast, 'Pattern 02' turns its attention to a female rap anthem, adding sections of the acapella to a moody, dubby, effects-laden and percussion-rich house groove smothered in mind-mangling, Ricardo Villalobos like effects. It's genuinely brilliant and far more experimental and thrillingly out-there than the Mode-ish A-side. … Read more

 Wishing You Were Here (Joey Negro extended mix) (8:07)

 How Deep Is Your Love (Timmy Regisford remix - Shelter vocal mix) (8:48)
 Review: Italian reissue specialists Groovin' Records continue to offer up archival gold. This time round, they've taken a dive into the bulging back catalogue of legendary New Jersey garage-house duo Blaze, return with two passes on 1999 single 'Wishing You Were Here'. On side A, we get Dave Lee's take (as Joey Negro, an alias he retired some years back), a deliciously organic chunk of gently breezy soulful house marked out by smooth vocals, jazzy bass guitar and even jazzier electric piano. Over on the flip, Blaze's old friend Timmy Regisford takes the controls. Reaching for squelchy acid bass, tighter beats and simmering synth-strings, the NYC house pioneer delivers a take that adds toughness to the long-serving twosome's looser and more light-touch original. … Read more

 Dave On Guitar
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1178142-01-02-02.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1178142-01-02-02.mp3] 
 Come Get I (feat 6LDA)
 Review: BRS aka British Rhythm Services, really do warrant the tag of deep house pioneers. They lay down foundations for a sound that are still being built on a quarter of a century later. They dropped several vital Eps, not least this one from 2000, originally on Cyclo Records. 'Shuff (60's Jazz mix)' kicks off with humid bass and pads and kick that move with a cool swing and skip. 'Outer Space (feat 6LDA)' lifts its head a little and slows in some late afternoon sun and intimate vocals whispers while 'Dave On Guitar' has lush licks and scary effects marbling a groove that means business. 'Come Get I (feat 6LDA)' has a more percussive looseness and sparkling keys. Jazz, garage and lounge vibes all get choked into the mix on this EP and the results are magnificent. … Read more

 Nu Acid
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1175571-01-02-02.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1175571-01-02-02.mp3] 
 Thiz Mood
 Review: Relaunched NYC label Nu Groove has secured the services of some genuine heavy hitters here, as old pals Steve Bug (the DJ/producer behind a slew of labels including Poker Flat) joins forces with another storied veteran of the Berlin scene, Stefan Braatz. To add an extra layer of legends, on side A's 'Circles' the pair are also joined by Chicago acid pioneers Virgo Four. They drop two versions of this deep and nostalgic treat: the warm, woozy and spoken word vocal-sporting original mix (A1) and the wonderfully jacking, stripped-back Beat Mix'. Should you need any more persuading to click 'add to cart', Bug and Braatz offer up two more killer cuts on side B, with self-explanatory jacker 'Nu Acid' being followed by the late 80s Windy City style acid house jauntiness of 'Thiz Mood'. … Read more

 Serotone (8:36)
 Review: 25 years into digging through music from every angle, Chillum Trio turns up on Faith In Truth with 'Driftlines', his third EP on vinyl. It's a four-track trip through the dubbier end of house, and it never sits still in one lane for too long. 'Dustfire' is a night drive with a Rhodes hook flickering through the haze. 'Deepskin' starts loose and humid, then tightens into garage-house brilliance. Flip it for 'Thin Air,' which is all phase-soaked horns and gentle acid, before 'Serotone' slips into jazzy, trip-hop-tinged downtempo to close. Eclectic, warm and built club-ready but with an alluring sense of drift. … Read more

 The Coastal Commission - "The Conversation" (7:24)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1143422-01-02-01.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1143422-01-02-01.mp3] 
 Boing - "Currency" (7:32)
 Review: More from the archives of cult Californian label Pacific Coast House Recordings, an imprint originally operative in the early-to-mid 2000s and now back via a series of carefully curated reissues. To kick things off, we get another classic from label stalwart 'Big' Sam Robson aka The Coastal Commission, 2000's 'Conversation'. Typical of West Coast deep house from the period - dreamy, tech-tinged, effects-laden and pleasingly spacey, with deliciously dubby bass and plenty of out-there noises - the track still sounds fresh and current despite its vintage. Turn over for an obscurity from Argentinian producer Boing, 'Currency', a cut that has never before been officially released on wax (it initially appeared on Robson's 2005 Pacific Coast House mix CD). Similarly dubby and intergalactic in tone, it boasts more propulsive beats and some lovely, dub techno-inspired synth riffs. … Read more

 Danny Kane - "Don't You Worry" (feat Colonel Red - Michael Gray extended mix)
 Review: For the uninitiated, Toolroom chief Mark Knight founded the Fool's Paradise offshoot as a platform for nostalgic music inspired by soulful and vocal house of the 1990s, as well as the earlier disco and boogie wave. It's fun, colourful and party-ready music for the most part, as the imprint's eighth label sampler proves. Check first DJ Fudge's glossy, filter-rich, hands-aloft 90s house roller 'Carry Us Away', before admiring Soul Avengerz chunky, pretty and emotion-stirring cover of Phase II's 1988 garage-house classic 'Reachin'. Over on the flip, Michael Gray and Dr Packer rework Sparque's West End Records' boogie-era classic 'Let's Go Dancing', reframing it as a loopy boogie-house gem, while Gray delivers a cheery and memory-stirring revision of Danny Kane's Colonel Red-voiced soulful house jam 'Don't You Worry'. … Read more

 My Love Turns To Liquid (Cozmic remix instrumental)
 Review: Way back in 2012, Rush Hour reissued Ben 'Cozmo D' Cenac and Gregg Fore's sole, self-titled album as Dream 2 Science - a glorious collection of heady deep house brilliance headed up by near perfect vocal number 'My Love Turns To Liquid'. Now they've returned to the duo's slim catalogue and reissued the 19909 12" version of that killer cut. The astonishingly good 'Original Mix' - the very same version showcased on the album - comes backed by a quartet of remixes and DJ tools. On side A you'll find the tactile, aquatic and subtly percussive, dub mix style 'Warm Water mix' and the synths-and-vocals 'Padapella'. Over on the reverse, Cenac doffs a cap to early UK bleep and bass and sub-heavy 1990 NYC deep house cuts on the superb 'Cozmic remix' and 'Cozmic remix instrumental'. … Read more

 Review: Bucharest's main minimal man Guy From Downstairs steps into the Pleasure Zone with 'Layers Only', where bespoke trim-out tricks and hotwired shuffles move move like the off-hours work of a master magician turned electrician. Active across Europe's minimal and tech house circuit since the mid-2010s, Downstairs's built a reputation for supple, late-night grooves on imprints like Robsoul and Hoga Nord while running his own GFD label back home; 'Layers Only' digs into that trademark Rominimal, each component clasping and clutching with clipped percussion, elastic bass and tentatively teased atmospherics: 'Don't Change' gets off on light foot with wildout circuit-janks turned beats, while 'Dream Untitled' hits heavier with its its waterlogged perc beds and quicksand kicks. … Read more

 Lights Out (alternative mix)
 Review: Unusually for a track called 'Lights Out', Cycle Records founder Jan Kincl's latest single is not dark, foreboding and driving, but rather an excellent exploration of the more colourful, musically expansive and sonically positive end of the deep house spectrum. His original mix (A1) is utterly gorgeous and sun-splashed, with an ability to lift the mood of all but the grumpiest of listeners. Kincl delivers an 'alternative mix' too (B2), which goes even deeper and draws even more sonic sunshine from his colourful riffs and chords. The other two revisions come from 21st century deep house don Trinidadian Deep. His 'Remix 1' take is more cosmic and percussion-rich, in line with his signature sound, while the 'Remix 2' version is spacey and synth-laden. … Read more

 Ossios & Rubinskee - "Drama Is A Game" (7:43)
 Review: Oscar Bernal aka Ossios is a multidisciplinary artist, DJ, and record collector based in Berlin who weaves ambient, global grooves, disco and more into his musical odysseys. He makes his Yay Recordings debut with music written on summer excursions and between flights, caught in fleeting bursts of inspiration. 'One Night at the Ranch (Betting the Farm)' comes with vocals from Elle: tumbling drums, understated grooves and a slow house hypnosis that unfolds without effort. Flip it for 'Drama Is a Game,' a hazy Mexico City afternoon of shape-shifting synths and warm, drifting vocals that dwells on memory and duality. It sounds like a very good summer, remembered late at night. … Read more

'More Than This' is our favourite, braving record-scratching 2-step slowed to a compelling snail's pace, and bearing no resemblance to the Roxy Music hit (unless you're hallucinating). … Read more

 Review: Multi-Culti's hush-hush Cult Edits series has been offering up sporadic collections of reworks since 2018, though surprisingly this tenth volume is the first for over two years.  Deep Sense Of (6:02)
 Review: Some 12 years into his career as a releasing artist, Platform Records founder Valerio Vaudano makes his bow on Chiwax with the first of what promises to be a series of EPs for the well-regarded German imprint. As you'd expect from a series titled The Sampling Theory, the observant amongst us will note plenty of nods to classic deep house cuts of old. Check first opener 'K 4 Kimchu', a pleasingly loose-limbed and tactile affair that blurs the boundaries between US garage-house and vintage Italian deep house, before admiring the sub0heavy, piano-laced chunkiness of 'It Excites Me'. 'I Luv Ya' is a heavier, deeper and darker chunk of muscular garage-house with added warehouse-ready riffs, while 'Deep Sense Of' updates classic early 90s Italo-house for the Tik-Tok generation. … Read more

 Colourful Karma - "For The Music" (feat Terra Deva - Mandrax dub) (6:36)
 Review: Two years ago, Phonica and Wolf Music associate Lea Lisa - a renowned dusty-fingered crate-digger - impressed with her first compilation of buried house treasures and overlooked recent releases, the simply titled Many Shades of House. Happily, this welcome follow-up is every bit as alluring as its predecessor, with the long-serving Brit offering up a mix of spacey late-night deep house soul (Karsakow's 'Sun of a Gun'), wide-eyed and analogue rich dancefloor dreaminess (the subtly acid-flecked 'Backside 50' by Cab Drivers), sunrise-ready psychedelic breaks (Fred Quest's 'Only On The Condition You Call Me Estaban'), ace Parisian dub house (Chateau Flight), organ-powered New Jersey garage-house (Funk Force Project) and rolling vocal deep house excellence (Colourful Karma). … Read more
